\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{ind}\text{-}\mathsf{msg}}
-indistinguishability from random anti-piracy and
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{identical}\text{-}\mathsf{cipher}}
-indistinguishability from random anti-piracy by exploiting the corresponding simultaneous inner product conjectures (see
15
and
14
).

\mathsf{Enc}\mathsf{Post}\mathsf{Process}(m,r)
:

1.

Sample
u\xleftarrow{\$}\mathbb{Z}_{Q}^{\lambda}
, where
Q
is the smallest prime number greater than
2^{|m|+\lambda}
, and
|m|
is the bit-size of the binary string
m
.

2.

Generate
\tilde{m}\leftarrow\mathsf{Embed}_{Q}(m)
, where
\mathsf{Embed}_{Q}
randomly embeds the binary string
m
in
\mathbb{Z}_{Q}
, i.e.,
\tilde{m}_{Q}\equiv kM+m_{Q}
where
k\xleftarrow{\$}\{0,1,\ldots,L-1\}
,
M\equiv 2^{|m|}
,
L\equiv Q\%M
, and
m_{Q}
is the canonical embedding of
m
in
\mathbb{Z}_{Q}
.

3.

Output
u,\tilde{m}_{Q}+\langle u,r\rangle
, where the addition and inner product uses the product over the field
\mathbb{Z}_{Q}
.

\mathsf{Dec}\mathsf{Post}\mathsf{Process}(c,r)
:

1.

Interprete
c
as
u,z
.

2.

Generate
\tilde{m}_{Q}\leftarrow z+\langle u,r\rangle
.

3.

Output
m
where
m
is the binary representation of
\tilde{m}_{Q}\mod M
.

Figure 6:
Construction of a
\mathsf{CLLZ}\text{ }{post}\text{-}{processing}

\mathsf{SDE}
scheme.

Remark 16
.

By the definition of the randomized embedding
\mathsf{Embed}_{Q}
defined in the algorithm
\mathsf{Enc}\mathsf{Post}\mathsf{Process}
given in
Figure

6
, it is easy to see that the ensemble

\{\mathsf{Embed}_{Q}(m)\}_{m\xleftarrow{\$}\{0,1\}^{M}}=\{\tilde{m}_{Q}\}_{\tilde{m}_{Q}\xleftarrow{\$}\{0,1,\ldots,LM-1\}}\approx_{s}\{\tilde{m}_{Q}\}_{\tilde{m}_{Q}\xleftarrow{\$}\mathbb{Z}_{Q}},

because
Q-L<M
by definition of
L
, and hence,
\frac{Q-L}{Q}<\frac{M}{Q}
which is at most
\frac{M}{M\cdot 2^{\lambda}}=\frac{1}{2^{\lambda}}
, by our choice of
Q
.

.

\mathsf{SDE}
as defined in
Figure

5
given in
Figure

6
satisfies
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{ind}\text{-}\mathsf{msg}}
-indistinguishability from random anti-piracy (see
Section

A.2
).

The only change from

was the distribution on

and

, which are independently and identically distributed in both the hybrids. In particular, the IID distribution on

and

across thre hybrids. Since these distributions are statistically indistinguishable by

, we conclude that statistically indistinguishability holds for
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{0}
and
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{1}
.

Consider the following independent search experiment against a pair of (uniform) efficient adversaries
\mathcal{B}^{\prime},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.

1.

\mathsf{Ch}
samples
r^{\mathcal{B}},r^{\mathcal{C}}\xleftarrow{\$}\mathbb{Z}_{Q}^{\lambda}
.

2.

\mathsf{Ch}
computes
\sigma_{\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}}
as follows:

(a)

Sample
(\mathsf{sk},\mathsf{pk})\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
and prepares
\rho_{k}\leftarrow\mathsf{QKeyGen}(k)
.

(b)

Run
\mathcal{A}(\rho_{k},\mathsf{pk})
to get
\sigma_{\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}}
.

3.

\mathsf{Ch}
computes
{c^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{B}}\leftarrow\mathsf{CLLZ}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},r^{\mathcal{B}})
, and computes
{c^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{C}}\leftarrow\mathsf{CLLZ}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},r^{\mathcal{C}})
.

4.

\mathsf{Ch}
constructs the bipartite auxiliary state
\tau^{r^{\mathcal{B}},r^{\mathcal{C}}}_{B,C}={c^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{B}},\sigma_{\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}},{c^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{C}}
, i.e., the
{c^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{B}},\sigma_{\mathcal{B}}
and
{c^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{C}},\sigma_{\mathcal{C}}
are the two partitions.

5.

\mathsf{Ch}
sends the respective registers of
\tau^{r^{\mathcal{B}},r^{\mathcal{C}}}_{B,C}
to
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, and gets back the responses
{r^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{B}}
and
{r^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{C}}
respectively.

6.

Ouptput
1
if
{r^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{B}}=r^{\mathcal{B}}
, and
{r^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{C}}=r^{\mathcal{C}}
.

Clearly, the winning probability of
(\mathcal{B}^{\prime},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
in the above game is the same as the winning probability of
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}^{\prime},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
in the independent search anti-piracy (see
Section

A.2
) of the
\mathsf{CLLZ}

\mathrm{single\ }\allowbreak\mathrm{decryptor\ }\allowbreak\mathrm{encryption}
scheme given in
Figure

4
. It was shown in
[
CLLZ21
, Theorem 6.15]
that the
\mathsf{CLLZ}

\mathrm{single\ }\allowbreak\mathrm{decryptor\ }\allowbreak\mathrm{encryption}
satisfies independent search anti-piracy assuming the security guarantess of post-quantum sub-exponentially secure
\mathsf{iO}
and one-way functions, and quantum hardness of Learning-with-errors problem (LWE). Hence, under the security guarantees of the above assumptions, there exists a negligible function
\epsilon^{\prime}()
such that the winning probability of
(\mathcal{B}^{\prime},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
in the above game is
\epsilon^{\prime}(\lambda)
. Since the order of the field
Q>2^{\lambda}
, assuming
15
, there exists a negligible function
\epsilon()
such that the winning probability of
(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})
in the following indistinguishability game is at most
\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon(\lambda)
.

1.

\mathsf{Ch}
samples
r^{\mathcal{B}},r^{\mathcal{C}}\xleftarrow{\$}\mathbb{Z}_{Q}^{\lambda}
.

2.

\mathsf{Ch}
computes
\sigma_{\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}}
as follows:

(a)

Sample
(\mathsf{sk},\mathsf{pk})\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
and prepares
\rho_{k}\leftarrow\mathsf{QKeyGen}(k)
.

(b)

Run
\mathcal{A}(\rho_{k},\mathsf{pk})
to get
\sigma_{\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}}
.

3.

\mathsf{Ch}
computes
{c^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{B}}\leftarrow\mathsf{CLLZ}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},r^{\mathcal{B}})
, and computes
{c^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{C}}\leftarrow\mathsf{CLLZ}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},r^{\mathcal{C}})
.

4.

\mathsf{Ch}
constructs the bipartite auxiliary state
\tau^{r^{\mathcal{B}},r^{\mathcal{C}}}_{B,C}={c^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{B}},\sigma_{\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}},{c^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{C}}
, i.e., the
{c^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{B}},\sigma_{\mathcal{B}}
and
{c^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{C}},\sigma_{\mathcal{C}}
are the two partitions.

5.

\mathsf{Ch}
samples
b\xleftarrow{\$}\{0,1\}
.

6.

\mathsf{Ch}
samples
u^{\mathcal{B}}\xleftarrow{\$}\mathbb{Z}_{Q}^{\lambda}
and compute
c^{\mathcal{B}}_{b}=(u^{\mathcal{B}},\langle u^{\mathcal{B}},r^{\mathcal{B}}\rangle)
if
b=0
, else samples
\tilde{m}^{\mathcal{B}}_{Q}\xleftarrow{\$}\mathbb{Z}_{Q}
, and computes
c^{\mathcal{B}}_{b}=(u^{\mathcal{B}},m^{\mathcal{B}}+\langle u^{\mathcal{B}},r^{\mathcal{B}}\rangle)
if
b=1
.

7.

Similarly,
\mathsf{Ch}
samples
u^{\mathcal{C}}\xleftarrow{\$}\mathbb{Z}_{Q}^{\lambda}
and computes
c^{\mathcal{C}}_{b}=(u^{\mathcal{C}},\langle u^{\mathcal{C}},r^{\mathcal{C}}\rangle)
if
b=0
, else samples
\tilde{m}^{\mathcal{C}}_{Q}\xleftarrow{\$}\mathbb{Z}_{Q}
, and computes
c^{\mathcal{C}}_{b}=(u^{\mathcal{C}},m^{\mathcal{C}}+\langle u^{\mathcal{C}},r^{\mathcal{C}}\rangle))
if
b=1
.

8.

\mathsf{Ch}
sends
c^{\mathcal{B}}_{b}
and
c^{\mathcal{C}}_{b}
along with the respective registers of
\tau^{r^{\mathcal{B}},r^{\mathcal{C}}}_{B,C}
to
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
respectively, and gets back the responses
b^{\mathcal{B}}
and
b^{\mathcal{C}}
respectively.

9.

Output
1
if
b_{\mathbf{B}}=b_{\mathbf{C}}=b
.

However, note that the view of the adversaries
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{C}
in the indistinguishability game above is the same as the view in
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{3}
. Therefore, the winning probability of
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})
in
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{1}
is at most
\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon(\lambda)
. This completes the proof of the theorem.

∎

and

Lemma 20
.

Assuimng post-quantum sub-exponentially secure
\mathsf{iO}
and quantum hardness of Learning-with-errors problem (LWE), the
\mathsf{CLLZ}

\mathrm{single\ }\allowbreak\mathrm{decryptor\ }\allowbreak\mathrm{encryption}
(see
Figure

4
) satisfies
{\sf Id}_{{\cal U}}
-search anti-piracy (see
Section

A.2
).

Proof.

By
[
CLLZ21
, Theorem 6.15]
, assuming the security of post-quantum sub-exponentially secure
\mathsf{iO}
and one-way functions, and quantum hardness of Learning-with-errors problem (LWE), the
\mathsf{CLLZ}

\mathrm{single\ }\allowbreak\mathrm{decryptor\ }\allowbreak\mathrm{encryption}
(see
Figure

4
) satisfies independent search anti-piracy.
Since the trivial success probabilities of the
{\mathcal{U}}
-search anti-piracy and
{\sf Id}_{{\cal U}}
-search anti-piracy expeirments for
\mathrm{single\ }\allowbreak\mathrm{decryptor\ }\allowbreak\mathrm{encryption}
are both negligible, by the lifting result in
[
AKL23
, Theorem ]
, we conclude that
Lemma

20
holds.
∎

5.2
Copy-Protection for PRFs with Preponed Security

We first introduce the definition of
{preponed\ security}
in
Section

5.2.1
and then we present the constructions of copy-protection in
Section

5.2.2
.

5.2.1
Definition

We introduce a new security notion for copy-protection called
{preponed\ security}
.

Consider a pseudorandom function family
\mathcal{F}=\{\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\mathbb{N}}
, where
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}=\{f_{k}:\{0,1\}^{\ell(\lambda)}\rightarrow\{0,1\}^{\kappa(\lambda)}\ :\ k\in\{0,1\}^{\lambda}\}
. Moreover,
f_{k}
can be implemented using a polynomial-sized circuit, denoted by
C_{k}
.

Definition 21

(Preponed Security)
.

A copy-protection scheme
\mathsf{CP}=(\mathsf{CopyProtect},\mathsf{Eval})
for
\mathcal{F}
(
Section

A.1
) satisfies
\mathcal{D}_{\cal X}
-
{preponed\ security}
if for any
QPT

(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})
, there exists a negligible function
\mathsf{negl}
such that:

\Pr[\mathsf{PreponedExpt}^{\left(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}\right),\mathcal{F},{\mathcal{U}}}\left(1^{\lambda}\right)=1]\leq\frac{1}{2}+\mathsf{negl}.

where
\mathsf{PreponedExpt}
is defined in
Figure

7
.

We consider two instantiations of
\mathcal{D}_{\cal X}
:

1.

{\mathcal{U}}
which is the product of uniformly random distribution on
\{0,1\}^{\ell}
, meaning
x_{1},x_{2}\leftarrow{\mathcal{U}}(1^{\lambda})
where
x_{1},x_{2}\xleftarrow{\$}\{0,1\}^{\ell}
independently.

2.

{\sf Id}_{{\cal U}}
which is the perfectly correlatd distribution on
\{0,1\}^{\ell}
with uniform marginals, meaning
x,x\leftarrow{\sf Id}_{{\cal U}}(1^{\lambda})
where
x\xleftarrow{\$}\{0,1\}^{\ell}
.

\mathsf{PreponedExpt}^{\left(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}\right),\mathsf{CP},\mathcal{D}_{\cal X}}\left(1^{\lambda}\right)
:

1.

\mathsf{Ch}
samples
k\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
, then generates
\rho_{C_{k}}\leftarrow\mathsf{CopyProtect}(1^{\lambda},C_{k})
and sends
\rho_{f_{k}}
to
\mathcal{A}
.

2.

\mathsf{Ch}
samples
x^{\mathcal{B}},x^{\mathcal{C}}\leftarrow\mathcal{D}_{\cal X}(1^{\lambda})
,
b\xleftarrow{\$}\{0,1\}
. Let
y^{\mathcal{B}}_{1}=f(x^{\mathcal{B}}),y^{\mathcal{C}}_{1}=f(x^{\mathcal{C}})

, and
y^{\mathcal{B}}_{0}=y_{1},y^{\mathcal{C}}_{0}=y_{2}
where
y_{1},y_{2}\xleftarrow{\$}\{0,1\}^{\kappa(\lambda)}
.
\mathsf{Ch}
gives
(y^{\mathcal{B}}_{b},y^{\mathcal{C}}_{b})
to Alice.

3.

\mathcal{A}(\rho_{C_{k}})
outputs a bipartite state
\sigma_{\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}}
.

4.

Apply
(\mathcal{B}(x^{\mathcal{B}},\cdot)\otimes\mathcal{C}(x^{\mathcal{C}},\cdot))(\sigma_{\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}})
to obtain
(b_{\mathbf{B}},b_{\mathbf{C}})
.

5.

Output
1
if
b_{\mathbf{B}}=b_{\mathbf{C}}=b
.

Figure 7:
Preponed security experiment for copy-protection of PRFs with respect to the distribution
\mathcal{D}_{\cal X}
.

5.2.2
Construction

The
\mathsf{CLLZ}
copy-protection scheme is given in
Figure

8
.

Assumes:
Punctrable and extractable
\mathsf{PRF}
family
F_{1}=(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathsf{Eval})
(represented as
F_{1}(k,x)=\mathsf{PRF}.\mathsf{Eval}(k,\cdot)
) and secondary
\mathsf{PRF}
family
F_{2},F_{3}
with some special properties as noted in
[
CLLZ21
]

\mathsf{CopyProtect}(K_{1})
:

1.

Sample secondary keys
K_{2},K_{3}
, and
\{\{|{A_{i}}_{s_{i},{s^{\prime}}_{i}}\rangle\}_{i}\}
, and compute the coset state
\{\{|{A_{i}}_{s_{i},{s^{\prime}}_{i}}\rangle\}_{i}\}
.

2.

Compute
\tilde{P}\leftarrow\mathsf{iO}(P)
where
P
is as given in
Figure

11
.

3.

Output
\rho=(\tilde{P},\{\{|{A_{i}}_{s_{i},{s^{\prime}}_{i}}\rangle\}_{i}\})
.

\mathsf{Eval}(\rho,x)
:

1.

Interprete
\rho=(\tilde{P},\{\{|{A_{i}}_{s_{i},{s^{\prime}}_{i}}\rangle\}_{i}\})
.

2.

Let
x=x_{0}\|x_{1}\|x_{2}
, where
x_{0}=\ell_{0}
. For every
i\in[\ell_{0}]
, if
x_{0,i}=1
apply
H^{\otimes n}
on
|{A_{i}}_{s_{i},{s^{\prime}}_{i}}\rangle
. Let the resulting state be
|\psi_{x}\rangle
.

3.

Run the circuit
\tilde{C}
in superposition on the input registers
(X,V)
with the initial state
(x,|\psi_{x}\rangle)
and then measure the output register to get an output
y
.

Figure 8:
\mathsf{CLLZ}
copy-protection for
\mathsf{PRF}
s.

P
:

Hardcoded keys
K_{1},K_{2},K_{3},R^{0}_{i},R^{1}_{i}
for every
i\in[\ell_{0}]

On input
x=x_{0}\|x_{1}\|x_{2}
and vectors
v=v_{1},\ldots v_{\ell_{0}}
.

1.

If
F_{3}(K_{3},x_{1})\oplus x_{2}=x_{0}\|Q
and
x_{1}=F_{2}(K_{2},x_{0}\|Q)
:

Hidden trigger mode:
Treat
Q
as a classical circuit and output
Q(v)
.

2.

Otherwise, check if the following holds: for all
i\in\ell_{0}
,
R^{x_{0,i}}(v_{i})=1
(where
x_{0,i}
is the
i^{th}
coordinate of
x_{0}
).

Normal mode:
If so, output
F_{1}(K_{1},x)
where
F_{1}()=\mathsf{PRF}.\mathsf{Eval}()
is the primary pseudorandom function family that is being copy-protected. Otherwise output
\bot
.

Figure 9:
Circuit
P
in
\mathsf{CLLZ}
copy-protection of
\mathsf{PRF}
.

Construction of Copy-Protection.

Proposition 22
.

Assuming the existence of post-quantum
\mathsf{iO}
, and one-way functions, and if there exists a
\mathsf{CLLZ}\text{ }{post}\text{-}{processing}

\mathsf{SDE}
scheme that satisfies

A.2
, then the

[
CLLZ21
, Section 7.3]
(see
Figure

8
) satisfies
{\mathcal{U}}
-
{preponed\ security}
(
Definition

21
).

Proposition 23
.

Assuming the existence of post-quantum
\mathsf{iO}
, and one-way functions, and if there exists a
\mathsf{CLLZ}\text{ }{post}\text{-}{processing}

\mathsf{SDE}
scheme that satisfies
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{identical}\text{-}\mathsf{cipher}}
-indistinguishability from random anti-piracy, see
Section

A.2
, then the
\mathsf{CLLZ}

[
CLLZ21
, Section 7.3]
(see

8
) satisfies
{\sf Id}_{{\cal U}}
-
{preponed\ security}
(
Definition

21
).

Proof of
proposition

22
.

To prove the proposition, we adopt the proof of
[
CLLZ21
, Theorem 7.12, Appendix F]
.

We will start with a series of hybrids.
The changes are marked in
blue
.

\mathsf{Hybrid}_{0}
: Same as
\mathsf{PreponedExpt}^{\left(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}\right),\mathsf{CP},\mathcal{D}_{\cal X}}\left(1^{\lambda}\right)
(see Game
7
) where
\mathcal{D}={\mathcal{U}}
(see the definition in
Definition

21
) for the
\mathsf{CLLZ}
copy-protection scheme see
Figure

8
.

1.

\mathsf{Ch}
samples
K_{1}\leftarrow\mathsf{PRF}.\mathsf{Gen}(1^{\lambda})
and generates
\rho=(\{|{A_{i}}_{s_{i},{s^{\prime}}_{i}}\rangle\}_{i\in\ell_{0}},\mathsf{iO}(P))\leftarrow\mathsf{CLLZ}.\mathsf{QKeyGen}(K_{1})
, and sends
\rho
to
\mathcal{A}
.

\mathsf{SDE}

as the challenge ciphertext and

from

(d)

in the ciphertexts

\mathsf{iO}
guarantees, the view of the inside
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}
is the same as that in
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{3}
.

.

15

3.1.1

Lemmas

32
and
33
.
∎

Theorem 31
.

Assuming
14
, the existence of post-quantum sub-exponentially secure
\mathsf{iO}
and one-way functions, and the quantum hardness of Learning-with-errors problem (LWE),
there is a construction of
unclonable
puncturable
obfuscation
satisfying

-generalized

security (see Definition
10
), for any generalized keyed puncturable circuit class

and

.
∎

The construction is as follows.
In the construction given in
Figure

10
, the
\mathsf{PRF}
family
(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathsf{Eval})
satisfies the requirements as in
[
CLLZ21
]
and has input length
n(\lambda)
and output length
m
;
\mathsf{PRG}
is a length-doubling injective pseudorandom generator with input length
m
.

Assumes:

\mathsf{PRF}
family
(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathsf{Eval})
with same properties as needed in
[
CLLZ21
]
,
\mathsf{PRG}
,
\mathsf{CLLZ}
copy-protection scheme
(\mathsf{CopyProtect},\mathsf{Eval})
.

\mathsf{Obf}(1^{\lambda},W)
:

1.

Sample a random key
k\leftarrow\mathsf{PRF}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
.

2.

Compute
\mathsf{iO}(P),\{\{|{A_{i}}_{s_{i},{s^{\prime}}_{i}}\rangle\}_{i}\}\leftarrow\mathsf{CLLZ}.\mathsf{CopyProtect}(k)
.

3.

Compute
\tilde{C}\leftarrow\mathsf{iO}(C)
where
C=\mathsf{PRG}\cdot\mathsf{iO}(P)
.

4.

Compute
\mathsf{iO}(D)
where
D
takes as input
x,v,y
, and runs
C
on
x,v
to get
y^{\prime}
and outputs
\bot
if
y^{\prime}\neq y
or
y^{\prime}=\bot
, else it runs the circuit
W
on
x
to output
W(x)
.

5.

Output
\rho=(\{\{|{A_{i}}_{s_{i},{s^{\prime}}_{i}}\rangle\}_{i}\},\tilde{C},\mathsf{iO}(D))
.

\mathsf{Eval}(\rho,x)

1.

Interprete
\rho=(\{\{|{A_{i}}_{s_{i},{s^{\prime}}_{i}}\rangle\}_{i}\},\tilde{C},\mathsf{iO}(D))
.

2.

Let
x=x_{0}\|x_{1}\|x_{2}
, where
x_{0}=\ell_{0}
. For every
i\in[\ell_{0}]
, if
x_{0,i}=1
apply
H^{\otimes n}
on
|{A_{i}}_{s_{i},{s^{\prime}}_{i}}\rangle
. Let the resulting state be
|\psi_{x}\rangle
.

3.

Run the circuit
\tilde{C}
in superposition on the input registers
(X,V)
with the initial state
(x,|\psi_{x}\rangle)
and then measure the output register to get an output
y
. Let the resulting state quantum state on register
V
be
\sigma
.

4.

Run
\mathsf{iO}(D)
on the registers
X,V,Y
in superposition where registers
X,Y
are initialized to classical values
x,y
and then measure the output register to get an output
z
. Output
z
.

Figure 10:
Construction of a UPO scheme.

Lemma 32
.

The construction given in
Figure

10
satisfies
(1-\mathsf{negl})
-
\mathsf{UPO}
correctness for any generalized puncturable keyed circuit class in
\sf{P/poly}
for some negligible function
\mathsf{negl}
.

Proof of
Lemma

32
.

Let
W
be the circuit that is obfuscated, and let the resulting obfuscated state be
\rho=(\{\{|{A_{i}}_{s_{i},{s^{\prime}}_{i}}\rangle\}_{i}\},\tilde{C},\mathsf{iO}(D))
. We will show that for every input
x=(x_{0},x_{1},x_{2})
, the
\mathsf{Eval}
algorithm on
(\rho,x)
outputs
W(x)
except with negligible probability. Let
|\phi_{x}\rangle
be the state obtained after running the Hadamard operation on
\{\{|{A_{i}}_{s_{i},{s^{\prime}}_{i}}\rangle\}_{i}\}
(see
Item

2
of the
\mathsf{Eval}
algorithm in
Figure

10
).
It is easy to check that for every input
x
, by the correctness of
\mathsf{CLLZ}
copy-protection, running
\tilde{C}
that is generated as
\tilde{C}\leftarrow\mathsf{iO}(C)
on
(x,|\phi_{x}\rangle
in superposition, and then measuring the output register results in
y
which is equal to
\mathsf{PRG}(\mathsf{PRF}.\mathsf{Eval}(k,x))
, except with negligible probability. By the almost as good as new lemma
[
Aar16
]
, this would mean that the resulting quantum state
\sigma
which is negligibly close to
|\psi_{x}\rangle\langle\psi_{x}|
in trace distance. Hence, running
C
on
\sigma
in
Item

4
and inside
\mathsf{iO}(D)
in superposition and then checking if the output is equal to
y
in superposition (see
Item

4
of the
\mathsf{Eval}()
algorithm in
Figure

10
), must succeed and
\mathsf{iO}(D)
will output
W(x)
, except with negligible probability. Therefore, except with negligible probability,
\mathsf{Eval}(\rho,x)
outputs
W(x)
.
∎

Hidden trigger mode:

Q
as a classical circuit and output
Q(v)
.

2.

Otherwise, check if the following holds: for all
i\in\ell_{0}
,
R^{x_{0,i}}(v_{i})=1
(where
x_{0,i}
is the
i^{th}
coordinate of
x_{0}
).

Normal mode:
If so, output
F_{1}(K_{1},x)
where
F_{1}()=\mathsf{PRF}.\mathsf{Eval}()
is the primary pseudorandom function family that is being copy-protected. Otherwise output
\bot
.

Next, we give a reduction from
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{1}2
to the
{preponed\ security}
of the
\mathsf{CLLZ}
copy-protection (for the
\mathsf{PRF}
s with the required property having the key-generation algorithm
\mathsf{KeyGen}
as mentioned above) to finish the proof.The reduction does the following.

1.

R_{\mathcal{A}}
runs
\mathcal{A}
to get a circuit
f
and
g
.

2.

R_{\mathcal{A}}
on receiving the copy-protected
\mathsf{PRF}
,
\mathsf{iO}(P),\{|{A_{i}}_{s_{i},{s^{\prime}}_{i}}\rangle\}_{i}
and
u^{\mathcal{B}},u^{\mathcal{C}}
, computes
y^{\mathcal{B}}\leftarrow\mathsf{PRG}(u^{\mathcal{B}})
and
y^{\mathcal{C}}=\mathsf{PRG}(u^{\mathcal{C}})
, and creates the circuit
\tilde{C}\leftarrow\mathsf{iO}(C)
where
{C}=\mathsf{PRG}\cdot\mathsf{iO}(P)
.
R_{\mathcal{A}}
also creates
\mathsf{iO}(D)
where
D
on input
x,v,y
runs
{C}
on
x,v
to get
y^{\prime}
and outputs
\bot
if
y^{\prime}\neq y
or
y^{\prime}=\bot
, else if
y^{\prime}\in\{y^{\mathcal{B}}_{0},y^{\mathcal{C}}\}
outputs
g(x)
, else it runs the circuit
W_{s}
to output
W_{s}(x)
.
R_{\mathcal{A}}
runs
\mathcal{A}
on
\rho_{k},\mathsf{iO}(D)
and gets an output
\sigma_{\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}}
, it then sends the corresponding registers of
\sigma_{\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}}
to both
R_{\mathcal{B}}
and
R_{\mathcal{C}}
.

3.

R_{\mathcal{B}}
and
R_{\mathcal{C}}
receive
x^{\mathcal{B}}
and
x^{\mathcal{C}}
from the challenger and run the adversaries
\mathcal{B}(x^{\mathcal{B}},\cdot)
and
\mathcal{C}(x^{\mathcal{C}},\cdot)
respectively on
\sigma_{\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}}
, to get the outputs
b^{\mathcal{B}}
and
b^{\mathcal{C}}
respectively,.
R_{\mathcal{B}}
and
R_{\mathcal{C}}
output
1-b^{\mathcal{B}}
and
1-b^{\mathcal{C}}
, respectively.

Finally, we prove the indistinguishability of the hybrids to finish the proof.

Indistinguishability of hybrids

Claim 37
.

Assuming the security of
\mathsf{iO}
, hybrids
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{0}
and
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{1}
are computationally indistinguishable.

Proof of Claim
37
.

For any function
f
, let
{\cal I}_{f}
denote the image of
f
.
Since
{\cal I}_{\mathsf{PRG}}
is a negligible fraction of
\{0,1\}^{2m}
and
y^{\mathcal{B}}_{0},y^{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
were chosen uniformly at random, with overwhelming probability
y^{\mathcal{B}}_{0},y^{\mathcal{C}}_{0}\not\in{\cal I}_{\mathsf{PRG}}
and hence not in
{\cal I}_{{C}}
. Therefore with overwhelming probability over the choice of
y^{\mathcal{B}}_{0},y^{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
, any
(x,v,y)
that satisfies this check also satisfies
y\not\in\{y^{\mathcal{B}}_{0},y^{\mathcal{C}}_{0}\}
. Hence with overwhelming probability, if
y^{\prime}=y\neq\bot
, the penultimate check (
item

3
in
fig.

14
) will always fail, and therefore,
D_{0}
will always output
W_{s}(x)
. Hence with overwhelming probability,
D_{0}
has the same functionality in both the hybrids, and therefore by
\mathsf{iO}
guarantees, the indistinguishability of the hybrids holds.

∎

Claim 38
.

Assuming the pseudorandomness of
\mathsf{PRG}
, hybrids
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{1}
and
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{2}
are computationally indistinguishable.

Proof of Claim
37
.

The proof is immediate. ∎

Claim 39
.

Assuming the security of
\mathsf{iO}
, hybrids
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{2}
and
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{3}
are computationally indistinguishable.

Proof of Claim
39
.

The modification did not change the functionality of
D_{1}
in this hybrid compared to the previous hybrid by the definition of
W_{s,x^{\mathcal{B}},x^{\mathcal{C}},\mu_{\mathcal{B}},\mu_{\mathcal{C}}}
and the
\mathsf{Puncture}
algorithm associated with
\mathcal{W}
.Hence, the indistinguishability follows from the
\mathsf{iO}
guarantees.
∎

Claim 40
.

Assuming the security of
\mathsf{iO}
, hybrids
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{3}
and
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{4}
are computationally indistinguishable.

Proof of Claim
40
.

The indistinguishability follows by the
\mathsf{iO}
guarantees and the claim that with overwhelming probability, the functionalities of
\mathsf{PRG}\cdot\mathsf{iO}(P)
and
C
in this hybrid are the same. The proof of the claim is as follows.

In the proof of correctness
[
CLLZ21
, Lemma 7.13]
of the
\mathsf{CLLZ}
copy-protection scheme, it was shown that the probability over the keys for the secondary pseudorandom functions, that
x^{\mathcal{B}},x^{\mathcal{C}}
are in the hidden triggers, is negligible. Hence, with overwhelming probability over the secondary pseudorandom function keys,
(x^{\mathcal{B}},v)
and
(x^{\mathcal{C}},v)
will not satisfy the trigger condition for
P
and therefore, not run in the hidden-trigger mode
13
13

13

Note that this property depends only on the secondary keys
k_{2}
and
k_{3}
. Since, over the hybrids, we only punctured the primary key and not the two secondary keys, the same correctness guarantee holds in this hybrid as in the unpunctured case of hybrid
0
.
. Hence with the same overwhelming probability, the functionality of
P
will not change even if we skip the hidden trigger check for
\{x^{\mathcal{B}},x^{\mathcal{C}}\}
. Note that conditioned on the functionality does not change for
P
by skipping the check for
\{x^{\mathcal{B}},x^{\mathcal{C}}\}
, the functionality of
C
in
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{2}
and
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{3}
are the same. Hence, with overwhelming probability, the functionality of
{C}
in
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{3}
is the same as that of
\mathsf{PRG}\cdot\mathsf{iO}(P)
.
∎

Claim 41
.

Assuming the security of
\mathsf{iO}
, hybrids
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{4}
and
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{5}
are computationally indistinguishable.

Proof.

The indistinguishability holds because
P
was hardcoded directly only in the circuit in the circuit
C
in the previous hybrid, and in
C
, we never use the key
P
to evaluate on
\{x^{\mathcal{B}},x^{\mathcal{C}}\}
, and hence the functionality did not change even after we punctured the
\mathsf{PRF}
key hardcoded inside
P
in
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{5}
, due to the puncturing correctness of the
\mathsf{PRF}
. Hence the indistinguishability follows from the
\mathsf{iO}
guarantee since we did not change the functionality of
{C}
.
∎

Claim 42
.

Assuming the security of the pseudorandom function family
\mathsf{PRF}
, hybrids
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{5}
and
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{6}
are computationally indistinguishable.

Proof.

The proof is immediate.
∎

Claim 43
.

Assuming the pseudorandomness of
\mathsf{PRG}
, hybrids
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{6}
and
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{7}
are computationally indistinguishable.

Proof.

The proof is immediate.
∎

Claim 44
.

Assuming the security of
\mathsf{iO}
, hybrids
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{7}
and
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{8}
are computationally indistinguishable.

Proof.

We will show that the functionality of
D_{1}
did not change across the hybrids
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{7}
and
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{8}
(see
figs.

17
and
15
), and hence indistinguishability of the hybrids follows from the
\mathsf{iO}
guarantees.
Note that since
{C}
in
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{8}
satisfies
{C}(x^{\mathcal{B}},v^{\mathcal{B}})=y^{\mathcal{B}}
and
{C}(x^{\mathcal{C}},v^{\mathcal{C}})=y^{\mathcal{C}}

\forall v^{\mathcal{B}}\in V^{\mathcal{B}}
and
v^{\mathcal{C}}\in V^{\mathcal{C}}
, where
V^{\mathcal{B}}
(respectively,
V^{\mathcal{C}}
) is the set of all
v
such that
(x^{\mathcal{B}},v)
(respectively,
(x^{\mathcal{C}},v)
) passes the coset check in the normal mode (see
item

2
), respectively.
Moreover, the image of
{C}
restricted to
{\cal X}_{{C}}\setminus\left((x^{\mathcal{B}},v^{\mathcal{B}})\cup(x^{\mathcal{C}},v^{\mathcal{C}})\right)
, i.e.,

{\cal I}_{{{C}}_{{\cal X}_{{C}}\setminus(x^{\mathcal{B}},v^{\mathcal{B}})\cup(x^{\mathcal{C}},v^{\mathcal{C}})}}\subset{\cal I}_{\mathsf{PRG}\left(\{0,1\}^{m}\right)},

where
m
is the output length of the
\mathsf{PRF}
family,
(x^{\mathcal{B}},v^{\mathcal{B}})
(respectively,
(x^{\mathcal{C}},v^{\mathcal{C}})
) is the short hand notation for
\{(x^{\mathcal{B}},v)\mid w\in V^{\mathcal{B}}\}
(respectively,
\{(x^{\mathcal{C}},v)\mid w\in V^{\mathcal{C}}\}
). Since
{\cal I}_{{\cal I}_{\mathsf{PRG}}}
is a negligible fraction of
\{0,1\}^{2m}
,
{\cal I}_{{{C}}_{{\cal X}_{{C}}\setminus(x^{\mathcal{B}},v^{\mathcal{B}})\cup(x^{\mathcal{C}},v^{\mathcal{C}})}}
is also a negligible fraction of
\{0,1\}^{2m}
.
Since
y^{\mathcal{B}}_{1},y^{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
are sampled uniformly at random independent of the set
{\cal I}_{{{C}}_{{\cal X}_{{C}}\setminus(x^{\mathcal{B}},v^{\mathcal{B}})\cup(x^{\mathcal{C}},v^{\mathcal{C}})}}
, except with negligible probability,

y^{\mathcal{B}}_{1},y^{\mathcal{C}}_{1}\not\in{\cal I}_{{{C}}_{{\cal X}_{{C}}\setminus(x^{\mathcal{B}},v^{\mathcal{B}})\cup(x^{\mathcal{C}},v^{\mathcal{C}})}}.

Note that we did not change the description of
{C}
after
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{3}
, hence as noted in
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{3}
,

{C}(x^{\mathcal{B}},v)\in\{y^{\mathcal{B}}_{1},\bot\},\quad{C}(x^{\mathcal{C}},v)\in\{y^{\mathcal{C}}_{1},\bot\}.

Therefore, combining the last two statements, except with negligible probability, the preimage(s) of
y^{\mathcal{B}}_{1}
are of the form
(x^{\mathcal{B}},v)
, and the only non-
\bot
image of
x^{\mathcal{B}}
is
y^{\mathcal{B}}_{1}
, and similarly for
y^{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
and
x^{\mathcal{C}}
. Hence except with negligible probability, the check that
y^{\prime}=y\neq\bot
and
y\in\{y^{\mathcal{B}}_{1},y^{\mathcal{C}}_{1}\}
is equivalent to
y^{\prime}=y\neq\bot
and
x\in\{x^{\mathcal{B}},x^{\mathcal{C}}\}
. Therefore with overwhelming probability, the functionality of
D_{1}
in
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{7}
(see
fig.

15
) and in
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{8}
(see
fig.

17
) are the same.

∎

Claim 45
.

Assuming the pseudorandomness of
\mathsf{PRG}
, hybrids
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{8}
and
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{9}
are computationally indistinguishable.

Proof.

The proof is immediate.
∎

Claim 46
.

Assuming the puncturing security of the pseudorandom function family
\mathsf{PRF}
, hybrids
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{9}
and
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{10}
are computationally indistinguishable.

Proof.

The proof is immediate.
∎

Claim 47
.

Assuming the security of
\mathsf{iO}
, hybrids
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{10}
and
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{11}
are computationally indistinguishable.

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of Claim
41
.
∎

Claim 48
.

Assuming the security of
\mathsf{iO}
, hybrids
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{11}
and
\mathsf{Hybrid}_{12}
are computationally indistinguishable.

The proof is the same as that of Claim
40
.
∎

∎

Proof of
Lemma

36
.

The proof is the same as that of
Lemma

35
upto minor adaptations and hence we omit the proof.
